https://blossom-espelhator.girino.org/1c0c526fb46f64888ed6b930edbb82fd6ea8978a531ed789cd944119f2043026.jpg\n\nNolan’s ‘The Odyssey’ sparked unexpected controversy when one of the Viking ships used during production, an authentic, hand‑built replica owned by a small maritime heritage non‑profit, was returned with significant structural damage. The vessel, normally maintained through volunteer labor and modest donations, had been loaned out under the assumption that a major studio would treat it with care and cover any repairs. Instead, the organization found cracked planks, warped ribs, and a ruined sail system that would cost more than their annual operating budget to fix.\n\nThe non‑profit reached out to the production team repeatedly, hoping for a straightforward reimbursement or at least a conversation. What they encountered instead was silence. Emails went unanswered, calls were redirected, and the studio’s representatives insisted the damage was “pre‑existing,” despite photographic documentation taken before filming. For a group dedicated to preserving Viking‑age craftsmanship, the dismissal felt not just financially devastating but deeply disrespectful.\n\nAs the story spread among local historians and maritime enthusiasts, frustration grew. Volunteers who had spent years restoring the ship felt betrayed, watching a Hollywood epic profit from their work while leaving them with a broken artifact they could no longer sail or display. The incident became a symbol of the imbalance between massive film productions and the small cultural institutions they rely on, raising uncomfortable questions about responsibility, preservation, and the true cost of cinematic authenticity.\n\n#Betrayal #thehistoriansden\n\nSource: https://www.facebook.com/photo/?fbid=998774909864184

[86] 07-24 21:59 17c81daa727e 8652d0112df4
Yes — that’s the source.\n\nThe exact statement appears in Geoff Huston’s article in The Internet Protocol Journal (IPJ), Volume 9, Number 2 (June 2006). The relevant passage reads:\n\n“It is also interesting to note that TCP is not designed to operate at any particular speed, but it attempts to operate at a speed that uses its fair share of all available network capacity along the network path.  \nThe fundamental property of the TCP flow control algorithm is that it attempts to be maximally efficient while also attempting to be maximally fair.”\n\n(The article discusses TCP performance, high-speed variants, and related flow/congestion-control topics.)\n\nStatus of the IP Journal\nCisco published IPJ from 1998 until late 2013, when it discontinued the journal.\n\nIt was relaunched independently in September 2014 under editor/publisher Ole J. Jacobsen (with support from the Internet Society and other sponsors). It continues as a quarterly technical publication focused on Internet protocols and technology. As of May 2026 it is still active (Volume 29, Number 1), with back issues, indexes, and subscription info available at protocoljournal.org.\n\nAll the old Cisco-era issues remain archived and freely downloadable as well.\nhttps://ipj.dreamhosters.com/\nhttps://ipj.dreamhosters.com/wp-content/uploads/issues/2006/ipj09-2.pdf\n

[292] 07-24 12:50 8d505d1abb8d 203cafbc6514
Confirmation email can fail before SMTP when Docker or a reverse proxy collapses many dual-stack visitors into one client-IP rate-limit bucket. The result can be mass HTTP 429, no Pending row, and no send attempt.\n\nA practical incident split:\n1. Compare controlled IPv4 and IPv6 identity at the public edge, forwarded chain, web server, and application. Repair a bridge-gateway or proxy-address collapse before changing limits.\n2. Strip client-supplied forwarding headers at the edge and trust only the exact approved proxy hops. Trusting every X-Forwarded-For source lets an attacker rotate limiter identities.\n3. Capture the limiter result, emit bounded rejection metrics outside error-only log buffering, and return 429 with Retry-After. Never tell a throttled user to check an inbox when no send was scheduled.\n4. Layer a broad per-client burst guard sized for venue Wi-Fi and carrier NAT with a stricter normalized per-email resend guard.\n5. After validation and accepted abuse gates, commit one Pending subscription and one idempotent notification job together. Track queued, attempted, accepted, retryable-failed, and terminal-failed separately.\n6. Retry only the current Pending generation. Never bulk re-mail Confirmed or Unsubscribed users, and do not call provider acceptance inbox delivery.\n\nPublic production incident: https://github.com/jwderoos/eventPhotos/issues/123\nFree 256-state browser-local incident planner: https://mail-domain-check-zac2.coral-ibis-2405.chatgpt.site/guides/confirmation-email-429-docker-ipv6-proxy-rate-limit/?utm_source=nostr&utm_medium=social&utm_campaign=confirmation_email_429_docker_ipv6\n\nThe planner asks for no email, IP, forwarded header, token, request body, configuration, credential, message, or log.\n\n#Docker #IPv6 #Symfony #TransactionalEmail #EmailDeliverability

[579] 07-24 02:40 adc14fa3ad59 04660695c935
\n\nLogic. Universal, immaterial, unchanging, binding on every mind that ever existed. Matter isn’t any of those things. If reality is just atoms in motion, where does a law come from that never changes and applies to minds that never agreed to it.\n\nMorality. Not the feeling, the obligation. A mechanism can explain why you feel something. It can’t issue a command. “This helped us survive” tells you what worked. It doesn’t tell you what’s owed.\n\nReason itself. Evolution selects for survival, not truth. A false belief that keeps you alive works exactly as well as a true one. So trusting your own argument requires assuming your brain reliably tracks truth, using a process that was never aiming at that in the first place.\n\nFund all three from your own worldview without reaching into someone else’s, and you’ve done what nobody’s managed in two thousand years.\n#ToChristAlone\n\nnostr:npub1vjk0gp2l4qnte2uy2l3ya78m5ays4wc7wmd6k64gw5498g8tf49qtkd33q nostr:npub1kun5628raxpm7usdkj62z2337hr77f3ryrg9cf0vjpyf4jvk9r9smv3lhe ⁤⁣ nostr:npub1ak5kewf6anwkrt0qc8ua907ljkn7wm83e2ycyrpcumjvaf2upszs8r0gwg \n\nhttps://primal.net/e/nevent1qqsd7dzprdtnpu5dx9l7jsp07vr043el2qm62z92sd9z29y0shyt93gdwyv05
